### Objection Handling — Recruitment (Telemarketer)

**Audience:** recruitment · **Role:** telemarketer · **Tags:** recruitment, objection-handling, faq

**Version 1 (Catalyst Recruitment — All Objections)**

> ## 🛡️ Recruitment Objection Handling
> 
> **Available timings:** Thursday 5:30pm, Friday 2pm, or Saturday 10am
> 
> ---
> 
> ### 1️⃣ "Not interested"
> 
> > I understand, but I think there's no harm going for the group interview, just to find out more! There are actually a total of 4 rounds of interviews, so the first round is just a briefing.
> >
> > Don't worry, it's just a short Zoom session to help you understand more about internship opportunities as well as your career options — and it's just a 20-min session out of your 24 hours a day.
> >
> > So would **[date and time]** work for you?
> 
> ---
> 
> ### 2️⃣ "Is this compulsory?"
> 
> > No, however most young adults who have already attended this session found it to be very beneficial right after they attended. So it will be beneficial for you to attend now that you have finished NS.
> >
> > We can set a tentative date on say **[date and time]** — which is more convenient for you? Do try to attend as time slots are specially allocated for you.
> 
> ---
> 
> ### 3️⃣ "What is the session about?"
> 
> > This would be a group briefing for you to learn more about the various job opportunities we currently have.
> >
> > So would **[date and time]** work better for you?
> 
> ---
> 
> ### 4️⃣ "Is this a sharing session? 1:1? Do you have a link?"
> 
> > Yes, this will be a group session — it'll be quite an interactive session to go through some of the potential career options for you, of which many other young adults have found it to be really beneficial for them.
> >
> > So would **[date and time]** work better for you?
> 
> ---
> 
> ### 5️⃣ "How did you get my number?"
> 
> > I'm not sure as it's in my system, but you might have placed your details on one of the job portals.
> 
> ---
> 
> ### 6️⃣ "Which organisation are you calling from?"
> 
> > I'm actually calling from Catalyst Recruitment — we are a recruitment agency.
> 
> ---
> 
> ### 7️⃣ "What is your name? I want to complain."
> 
> ⚠️ *At most give your first name. Do NOT give your contact or mobile number.*
> 
> > Hi, my name is [First Name]. I understand your frustration, however this is just a service call to check if you are keen to understand how to better explore your internship and career options. I apologise for the inconvenience caused, if any.
> 
> *Then **HANG UP** — the more you talk, the more they will try to find trouble.*

### Objection — "Not Interested in Insurance"

**Audience:** general · **Role:** consultant · **Tags:** objection-handling, insurance

*2 versions:*

**Version 1 (MoneyBees Script — V1 (Redirect to Planning))**

> **Q: I'm not interested in insurance.**
> 
> **A:** I understand where you are coming from. Most people that I speak to have the same sentiment and do not want to delve into this matter until much later.
> 
> However, most people also agree that financial planning is important and needs to be done regularly. This is why I would love to have a quick **30-minute Zoom call** for both of us to analyse this and see how we can plan for your success.
> 
> Is **[date, time]** or **[date, time]** good for you?

**Version 2 (MoneyBees Script — V2 (Pivot to Investing))**

> **Q: Not interested in insurance.**
> 
> I see, do you do your own investment?
> 
> **If Yes:** What do you invest in? What are some of the struggles or challenges you face currently?
> 
> **If No:** Are you looking to learn more about investments?
> 
> ---
> 
> **If still not interested:**
> 
> I see, can I ask why you are no longer interested?
> 
> > *Try to still add them into a nurturing machine — send resources, keep the relationship warm.*

### Fact-Finding — Insurance Coverage Check

> ### 🤷 If Not Sure:

### Handling Video-Off Objections (Zoom)

**Audience:** general · **Role:** consultant · **Tags:** objection, appointment, rapport, retirement

**Version 1 (Leo Tan)**

> - If they don't want to switch on the video (expand to see how to deal)
>   - "Typically for our Zoom sessions, the purpose of today's conversation is really to understand each other better, and to see if we might be a good fit to work together. That's why we encourage having the video on, as it helps in building trust and connection on both ends."
> "Since we're talking about something as personal as retirement planning, and also for courtesy sake, it's also good for us to put a face to the voice, it feels more secure, and allows us to engage both ways as well"
> "With that said, I completely understand if you're not comfortable with this arrangement. At the same time, I also want to respect both our time - so if turning on the video is not something you're okay with, it may be difficult for us to continue the conversation meaningfully, and we can perhaps call it off here with no hard feelings"

### Objection Handling — Cost of Delay (Time vs Money) - Pre-Retirees

**Audience:** working-adult · **Role:** consultant · **Tags:** objection, retirement, closing, savings, investment, pre-retirees

**Version 1 (Leo Tan)**

> Prospect is delaying decision
> - Idea: 
>   - At the end of the day, you are going to do something, could be investing yourself, could be buying a product, you will eventually do something. The question is just when will you do it. 
>   - The longer you delay, the greater the problem, and the greater the cost. You are already paying, just that you're paying with time instead of money. So when are you going to stop that?
> - Words to use:
>   - Mr. Prospect, would you agree that retirement in Singapore isn’t exactly cheap? (Yes)
>   - That said, if we’re aiming for a comfortable lifestyle in our retirement years, would you say it makes sense that we’ll probably need to set aside quite a bit to get there? (Yes)
>   - Over the years, working with many clients, I’ve noticed something quite consistent when it comes to building wealth. It really boils down to just two main ingredients.
> Would you like to guess what they might be? (Let them reply)
>   - They’re time and money. Let me explain a little — we've seen and built many retirement portfolios over the course of our careers. 
> For clients who started saving or investing early, say in their 20s or 30s, they didn’t need to set aside a lot each year. A few thousand dollars annually added up over time into six-figure portfolios.
>   - But for those who only started planning later, they had to contribute much more — sometimes tens of thousands — just to catch up.
>   - So in a way, to reach our goals, we’re always “paying” — either with time or with money. The real question is: how much of each are we willing or able to commit today?
>   - And I completely understand if you’re still exploring your options — that’s perfectly normal.
> But could it also be that, perhaps without realising, we’ve already been paying with time by waiting or postponing this?
>   - And if that continues, do you think it’s possible we might end up needing to contribute even more down the road… or potentially compromise on the lifestyle we’re working towards?

### Why do I need a Zoom call? Can't I just take the course?

**Audience:** general · **Role:** consultant · **Tags:** faq, objection-handling, zoom

**Version 1 (Standard Answer)**

> **Q: Why do I need a Zoom call? Can't I just take the course on my own?**
> 
> **A:** Great question! The Zoom session is actually what makes this programme so much more valuable than just a regular online course.
> 
> During the call, we'll:
> 
> ✅ Do a quick **financial health check** based on your current situation
> ✅ **Personalise** the course content to your specific goals and life stage
> ✅ Answer any questions you have about savings, investing, or financial planning
> 
> Think of it as a **free consultation** with a financial planning professional — something that normally costs money elsewhere. The course on its own is great, but the Zoom session helps you apply it to *your* life specifically 😊

>

> The earlier you start, even with small amounts, the more powerful **compound interest** works in your favour. That's exactly why we focus on young adults — time is your biggest asset! 💪

### General Calling Tips & Tonality

**Audience:** general · **Role:** consultant · **Tags:** tips, tonality, calling

**Version 1 (Team Guide)**

> ## 🗣️ Tonality & Language Tips
> 
> - **Don't use "ads"** — use words such as **"campaigns"** instead
> - Talk in a more **cheerful and friendly tone**
> - When giving dates or setting new dates for appointments, **take your own time** and try to make it sound like you're busy — this **creates scarcity**
> 
> ---
> 
> ## 📋 Objection Handling Reminder
> 
> - If they say "Not interested", always ask: **"May I ask why you will not be interested in the session?"**
> - Try to still set the call, especially if they are **on the fence**
> - Even if they decline, try to **add them into a nurturing machine** — send resources, keep the relationship warm
> - Never give up on the first objection — most people need a gentle push
> 
> ---
> 
> ## 📞 Consultant Callback Tips
> 
> - During rapport building, **add their birthday and milestones into your calendar** and mark their current stage in the CRM
> - Use **WhatsApp Business quick replies** when sending the resource overview during the callback call
> - Ask them to **save your number** so future resources get through — guide them to the "Save Contact" button at the top of WhatsApp
> - After the callback call, **change the lead stage** from "Texting" to **"To Meet for Sales"** so you know these are upcoming leads
> - Encourage **referrals** by giving them a ready-made script to share with friends under 25
> - Position the **Zoom consultation as the main value** and the free course as a **bonus** — not the other way around

---

### Texting EQ — The 11 Rules for Warm Outreach

**Audience:** warm-market · **Role:** consultant · **Tags:** texting-eq, tips, rulebook, warm-market

**Version 1 (Texting EQ Guide)**

> ### The Ultimate Texting Rulebook
> *From Texting EQ by Wilfred Wong*
> 
> **Tip 1:** Start the convo by linking it back to a previous conversation. Don't sound random.
> > "Hey bro the other time you were saying you work in Tanjong Pagar right? Exactly where is your office?"
> 
> **Tip 2:** Create enough interest with every text. Every text gives the prospect MORE REASON to meet us.
> > "I've been wanting to show you how I helped some of my clients to increase their investment returns with their existing portfolio."
> 
> **Tip 3:** End off every text with an easy-to-answer question.
> > "Are you working from home or office?"
> 
> **Tip 4:** Make them feel SAFE for the upcoming consultation.
> > "At the end of the day, sometimes I advise people not to invest at all if we see no need — when we meet we can discuss and you can decide for yourself."
> 
> **Tip 5:** Talk about common ground to ENCOURAGE the conversation.
> > "Some of my clients are also doctors and I help them with their investments since they usually work long hours."
> 
> **Tip 6:** Actively FOLLOW UP. 90% of the time, people fail to reply. Just give them a bump by name.
> > "Hi Sarah!"
> 
> **Tip 7:** Reply QUICKLY (immediately, or within 3-4 hours). It shows reliability and service.
> 
> **Tip 8:** Be CASUAL. Don't use complicated jargon.
> > ❌ Don't use words like "Optimisation"
> > ✅ Use emojis and "Hahaha" if it sounds like you!
> 
> **Tip 9:** Texting approaches work BEST after a few engagements/touchpoints beforehand. Touchpoints warm up the prospect.
> 
> **Tip 10:** Never use the word FREE. Instead:
> > "When is a better time for a 30 mins call? Would next Thursday or Friday be better?"
> 
> **Tip 11:** Minimise the prospect's thinking when setting CTA.
> > ❌ "When are you available next month?"
> > ✅ "For next month I have 10th Jan 6pm and 23rd Jan 8pm, which is better for you?"

---

### Phrasing Tips — What to Avoid & What to Say Instead

**Audience:** general · **Role:** consultant · **Tags:** tips, phrasing, communication

**Version 1 (Team Guide — Phrasing V1)**

> ## 🚫 Try to Avoid
> 
> These phrases put the prospect in a position to say **no** or create awkwardness:
> 
> | ❌ Don't Say |
> |---|
> | "Do you still recall?" |
> | "Are you interested?" |
> | "Are you free at this time?" |
> | "Are you free to speak to?" |
> | "Is this a convenient time to speak now?" |
> 
> ---
> 
> ## ✅ Try This Instead
> 
> Use **assumptive and confident language** that keeps the conversation moving forward:
> 
> | ✅ Say This Instead |
> |---|
> | "I understand that you are…" |
> | "This is a courtesy call to inform you…" |
> | "I'll just keep this call short — less than a minute" |
> | "We'll just set a tentative time slot at **[date/time]**, then after this call, I'll give you a WhatsApp, and you just need to reply and confirm — is that alright? We can reconfirm closer to the date." |
> 
> ---
> 
> 💡 **Key Principle:** Avoid asking **permission-based questions** that invite rejection. Instead, use **assumptive statements** that guide the prospect naturally into the next step.

---

### Callback Checklist — Step-by-Step Guide

**Audience:** young-adult · **Role:** consultant · **Tags:** tips, checklist, callback

**Version 1 (Team Guide)**

> ## ✅ Checklist for Callbacks (Young Adults Under 25)
> 
> ---
> 
> ### 1️⃣ Initial Introduction
> 
> - Start with the purpose: *"Hi [Name], this is [Your Name]. My assistant may have called you earlier, and I'm following up personally about an opportunity for young adults under 25."*
> - Confirm age: *"Just to confirm, are you under 25?"*
> 
> ---
> 
> ### 2️⃣ Build Rapport
> 
> - Ask about their life stage: *"Great! Are you currently studying, serving NS, or working? What's your current focus right now?"*
> - Gather key milestones: *"When's your birthday? Do you have any major milestones coming up, like ORD, graduation, or starting a new job?"*
> 
> ---
> 
> ### 3️⃣ Send Overview on WhatsApp
> 
> - Inform them: *"I'll be sending you an overview of what we'll be discussing via WhatsApp. Are you on WhatsApp now?"*
> - Confirm receipt: *"I've just sent it to you — can you check if you've received it?"*
> - Guide them to save your number: *"To make sure you receive all future resources, could you save my contact? Just scroll to the top of the message and click 'Save Contact.' Let me know once that's done!"*
> 
> ---
> 
> ### 4️⃣ Share the Heart of the Programme
> 
> - Explain the purpose: *"The reason this programme was created is that these topics aren't typically taught in school."*
> - Highlight key topics:
>   - Personal finance
>   - Productivity and self-improvement
>   - Entrepreneurship and investing in yourself — not just your money
> - Share team credibility: *"Our team has been doing this for over 10 years, and this year marks our 10th anniversary of helping young adults."*
> - Emphasise starting young: *"The earlier you start, the greater the ripple effect. Learning these things at 20 or 25 is so much more impactful than starting at 65."*
> 
> ---
> 
> ### 5️⃣ Explain the Zoom Call + Free Consultation
> 
> - Set expectations: *"During the Zoom call, I'll explain the different ways you can start growing your money and investing. At the end of the session, you'll also get access to the course for free."*
> - Personalised consultation: *"The Zoom call will also include a free personal financial consultation tailored to your current situation. I'll help you identify specific steps you can take based on your goals."*
> 
> ---
> 
> ### 6️⃣ Book the Appointment
> 
> - Suggest a time: *"Does next Saturday morning at 10am work for you? The call will only take about 30 minutes."*
> - Confirm: *"Great! I'll send you the Zoom link closer to the date. Please feel free to reach out if you have any questions before then."*
> 
> ---
> 
> ### 7️⃣ Encourage Referrals
> 
> - Ask for referrals: *"If you know anyone else under 25 who might benefit from this, feel free to share this with them."*
> - Give them a ready-made script:
>   > *"Hey, I came across a free financial consultation that helps young adults learn how to grow their money. They also provide access to a great course afterward. Let me know if you're interested!"*
> - Offer to include friends: *"If they're interested, they can join the Zoom call with you or book their own time."*
> 
> ---
> 
> ### 8️⃣ Close the Call
> 
> - Recap: *"To summarise, during the call we'll discuss ways to grow your money, go through a financial consultation, and give you access to the course for free."*
> - Thank them: *"Thank you for your time, [Name]! I'll see you next Saturday at 10am on Zoom. Have a great day!"*
> 
> ---
> 
> ## 🔑 Key Points to Remember
> 
> - Keep the tone **conversational, relatable, and enthusiastic**
> - Focus on the **financial consultation as the primary benefit**, with the course as a valuable bonus
> - **Personalise** the call by referencing their life stage and milestones
> - Always confirm they've **saved your number** and received the WhatsApp overview
> - Be **clear and concise** when explaining the purpose and setting expectations

### Warm Market — Outreach Flow (Step-by-Step)

**Audience:** warm-market · **Role:** consultant · **Tags:** framework, warm-market, step-by-step

**Version 1 (Framework)**

> ### Warm Market Outreach Flow
> 
> **Step 1 — Plant the seed (before you start)**
> > "I will become a FA soon"
> 
> Just share casually. No ask yet.
> 
> ---
> 
> **Step 2 — Make a soft ask for the future**
> > "When I become a FA, can I…"
> > - practice my pitch on you?
> > - share with you what I do?
> > - have a coffee session with you to share what I do?
> 
> 🎯 **Goal:** Let their guard down. You're not selling — you're asking for support.
> 
> ---
> 
> **Step 3 — Get a "yes" for something in the future**
> 
> They agree to something *later*, not now. No pressure.
> 
> ---
> 
> **Step 4 — Follow up when the time comes**
> 
> When you actually go back and say:
> > "Hey! Remember I mentioned I'd love to share what I do? I'm ready now — when works for you?"
> 
> By then they've already said yes. The meetup feels natural, not salesy.

---

### Double Your Calling Power — Two-Phone Strategy

**Audience:** general · **Role:** consultant · **Tags:** tips, productivity, two-phone

**Version 1 (Team Guide)**

> ## 📱📱 Double Your Calling Power: The Two-Phone Strategy
> 
> If you're serious about cold calling, use **two phones simultaneously**. It's basic maths that gives you an unfair advantage.
> 
> ---
> 
> ### 🛠️ Setup (2 Minutes)
> 
> - 2 phones (primary + a second device)
> - Lark on web mode on your computer
> - WhatsApp on web mode on your computer
> - Your prospect list ready to go
> 
> ---
> 
> ### ⚡ How It Works
> 
> 1. Dial **two numbers simultaneously** — one on each device
> 2. See which prospect picks up first
> 3. After 10 rings with no answer → cut both calls, move to next two numbers
> 4. If one picks up → immediately cut the other call, take the conversation
> 5. After the call → move to the next two rows on your list
> 
> > ⚠️ **Important:** Don't redial the number that didn't pick up — keep moving forward
> 
> ---
> 
> ### 📊 The Maths (In Your Favour)
> 
> Waiting time follows a Poisson distribution. Running two queues cuts waiting time by **30–40%**.
> 
> | Metric | Impact |
> |---|---|
> | 1 hour dual-phone | = 2 hours single-phone |
> | Weekly savings | 5+ extra hours |
> | Monthly savings | 20+ extra hours |
> | Yearly savings | **240+ hours (6 work weeks!)** |
> 
> ---
> 
> ### 🔑 The One Rule That Matters
> 
> **Always text AFTER your calling session ends — NOT after each individual call.**
> 
> Stopping to text after every call destroys your momentum. You're a machine during calling time. Texting comes later, in **batch mode**, after you've crushed your calling block.
> 
> ---
> 
> ### ❓ FAQ
> 
> **Q: Won't I get confused?**
> A: You will for the first 10 minutes. Then it becomes automatic.
> 
> **Q: What if both pick up?**
> A: Extremely rare. If it happens, cut one and take the other. Good problem to have.
> 
> **Q: Isn't it disrespectful to cut off?**
> A: They never knew you called. You hung up before they answered. Zero impact.
> 
> **Q: What about the person who didn't pick up?**
> A: Move on. Don't look back. Forward momentum beats second-guessing every time.
> 
> ---
> 
> ### 💡 Bottom Line
> 
> Every hour you call with one phone when you could be using two is an hour you're choosing to work at **50% capacity**.
> 
> Set it up today. Your future self — the one with a calendar full of appointments — will thank you.

---

### Physical Voucher Procedure & Lead Follow-Up

**Audience:** general · **Role:** consultant · **Tags:** tips, voucher, procedure

**Version 1 (Voucher SOP + Follow-Up Rules)**

> ## 🎫 Physical Voucher Procedure
> 
> ### Overview
> 
> Collate all the people that actually need the vouchers at the **end of the 4 months / end of campaign** and post it to them.
> 
> ---
> 
> ### 📋 Procedure (e.g. Yakun Leads)
> 
> > Regarding the Yakun leads — the vouchers will be **mailed out** to them. At the end of the 3 months, please collate all the people that you have met up with, then **mail out to them all at one shot**.
> 
> - If you **meet up with them physically** → yes, you can just pass it to them directly
> - If they are **super not qualified** and there's no need for a physical meeting → tell them that after 3 months, we will collate all the vouchers and **send them out all at once** (saves time)
> 
> ---
> 
> ### 📞 Lead Follow-Up Rules
> 
> | Scenario | Action |
> |---|---|
> | **Lead replies** | Give a **call immediately** to set an opening appointment **on the same day** |
> | **Lead does not reply** | Give a call after **2–3 days** to try and set an opening appointment |

---

### FYC Formula — Calling Rate & Activity Math

**Audience:** general · **Role:** consultant · **Tags:** tips, fyc, activity-math

**Version 1 (Team Guide — Activity Math V1)**

> ## 💰 FYC = How Many Cases You Close
> 
> > Your **FYC** (First Year Commission) is in **direct proportion** to how many cases you close.
> 
> ---
> 
> ### The Chain Reaction
> 
> | Step | Depends On |
> |---|---|
> | 💰 **FYC** | How many **cases you close** |
> | 📋 **Cases closed** | How many **appointments you set** |
> | 📞 **Appointments set** | Your **calling rate** and **calling duration** |
> 
> ---
> 
> ### 📊 The Numbers — Part-Time vs Full-Time
> 
> | Metric | Part-Time | Full-Time |
> |---|---|---|
> | **Dials per hour** | 10 | 70 |
> | **Sessions per week** | 2 | 40 hrs (8h × 5 days) |
> | **Dials per week** | **20** | **2,500** |
> 
> ---
> 
> 💡 **The takeaway:** Activity is the #1 driver of results. If you want more FYC, increase your **calling rate** and **calling duration** — everything else follows.

### Warm Market Outreach — Tips & Mindset

**Audience:** general · **Role:** consultant · **Tags:** tips, mindset, warm-market, warm-market

**Version 1 (Guidelines)**

> ### Warm Market Outreach Tips
> 
> **Mindset: Add value or seek help — don't aim to sell.**
> 
> Seek help and act pitiful a bit to lower their guard 🥹
> 
> ---
> 
> **Reframe the meetup for your prospect:**
> 
> 🟡 **Worst case** → They get a second opinion on their portfolio, greater assurance that they have a great portfolio, a financial report + a neater summary of all their policies
> 
> 🟢 **Best case** → They get to reduce their cost of insurance and free up cash flow for retirement

---

### Warm Market — What NOT to Do

**Audience:** general · **Role:** consultant · **Tags:** tips, warm-market, dos-donts, warm-market

**Version 1 (Guidelines)**

> ### ❌ What NOT to Do in Warm Market Outreach
> 
> **Don't be too direct with product pitching.**
> 
> 🚫 "We have an ILP that can help you to invest"
> 
> This immediately puts their guard up. You sound like every other agent they've been avoiding.
> 
> ---
> 
> **Instead:**
> - Lead with curiosity and help
> - Let them discover the value through the conversation
> - Your goal is to get the **meetup**, not to sell over text

---

### Texting EQ — Extra Success Pointers

**Audience:** general · **Role:** consultant · **Tags:** texting-eq, tips, warm-market, productivity, warm-market

**Version 1 (Texting EQ Guide)**

> ### Extra Pointers for Texting Success
> *From Texting EQ by Wilfred Wong*
> 
> **Pointer 1 — Keep an Excel tracker**
> Store names of people who didn't reply or gave objections. Once someone crosses your mind, add them. If you document every name and follow up, you will never run out of people to text.
> 
> **Pointer 2 — Split texts into chunks**
> Send multiple short messages instead of one long wall of text. It feels more casual and unscripted.
> 
> **Pointer 3 — Never start with "How are you?"**
> It's too random and deliberate — raises eyebrows about your intentions.
> > ❌ "How are you?"
> > ✅ "Hey XX, are you still working at Singtel?"
> 
> **Pointer 4 — Have fun!**
> Laugh off unfavourable replies, have confidence in your ability to impact the prospect, and SHOW this confidence in your texts.
> 
> ---
> 
> ### Bonus Tips for Setting Meetups:
> 
> 🗓️ **Set the date further away** — Suggest a date 1-2 months later. More freedom = higher chance of yes.
> 
> ⏳ **Postpone the scheduling** — "I'll text you in Feb to arrange a date with you?" Easy yes.
> 
> 📊 **Send visuals** — Pictures, graphs, case studies to illustrate your points.
